--- Commit Message ---
LU-10026 lustre: add lz4 and lz4hc kernel modules

lz4 and lz4hc kernel modules implement compression according to the
lz4 and lz4hc algorithms respectively, through the kernel Crypto API.
lz4 module provides 2 cipher drivers under the generic name 'lz4':
* lz4-lustre-generic of type compression
* lz4-lustre-scomp of type scomp
lz4hc module provides 2 cipher drivers under the generic name 'lz4hc':
* lz4hc-lustre-generic of type compression
* lz4hc-lustre-scomp of type scomp

lz4 and lz4hc kernel module sources are copied from linux v6.1-rc5,
and renamed to llz4.c and llz4hc.c respectively to avoid name
collisions. Use of vmalloc has been changed to kvmalloc since it is
faster in most cases. They implement the Crypto API interface, and
rely on the lz4/lz4hc kernel library for compression implementation.
They have been modified to grok a compression acceleration/level, as
read from the top 4 bits of the crypto_tfm flags, and pass it to the
underlying library.
The lz4/lz4hc library sources are also copied from linux v6.1-rc5 and
built statically, so lz4_compress, lz4_decompress and lz4hc_compress
sources have been "de-modulified", and EXPORT_SYMBOLs removed. Headers
have also been copied from linux v6.1-rc5 for consistency, and source
files modified to include the copied headers instead of the system
headers.

All aforementioned sources are located in the lustre_compat/crypto/lz4
directory.

The lz4/lz4hc modules are built only if the kernel does not provide
them. This is verified by checking if the CONFIG_CRYPTO_LZ4 and
CONFIG_CRYPTO_LZ4HC kernel config options are defined.

This patch provides unit testing of several compression modules, such
as lz4, lz4hc, lzo and gzip. This is done via a new test kernel module
kcompr.ko, and new sanity test_84. File lustre/tests/kernel/kcompr.c
contains examples of how to call the compression/decompression
routines.

EX-7998 lustre: Fix build with crypto_tfm_ctx on kernel 6.2

In recent kernels, commit e634ac4
"crypto: api - Add crypto_tfm_ctx_dma"
moved crypto_tfm_ctx into algapi.h. So this file must be included
from lustre_crypto.h.
Was-Change-Id: If10f721355da3e58ca541c17615e978334e8d718

EX-7683 utils: always try to use our own lz4/lz4hc

lz4/lz4hc provided by the kernel do not grok a compression level.
The built-in lz4/lz4hc do, so always build them as dedicated kernel
modules llz4.ko and llz4hc.ko, with the same .cra_name but with a
slightly higher .cra_priority = 110, so that they are preferred over
the in-kernel modules if any.

And try to manually load the llz4/llz4hc kernel modules when a file
requires compression with the corresponding alg. This is a "one-shot"
try that allows us to prefer our modules that has level support, but
continues to at least compress/decompress files even if our own
modules are not available.
Was-Change-Id: I0bdf267f998e21df81e460250a653aed34e3215d

EX-bug-id: EX-6275
Test-Parameters: testlist=sanity env=ONLY=84

--- Message (James Simmons, 2025-01-15 17:29:42.000000000) ---
Patch Set 3: Code-Review-1

The lz4 and lz4hc modules exist upstream already. Why are importing our own version of the same driver? Lets not get in the spot with fscrypt where we end up missing all the fixes upstream since we are doing our own thing again.

--- Message (Sebastien Buisson, 2025-01-15 17:44:04.000000000) ---
Patch Set 3:

> Patch Set 3: Code-Review-1
> 
> The lz4 and lz4hc modules exist upstream already. Why are importing our own version of the same driver? Lets not get in the spot with fscrypt where we end up missing all the fixes upstream since we are doing our own thing again.

James, there are several reasons why we would want to have our own copy of lz4:
- el8 does not support lz4 compression.
- even on el9, there is no support for lz4 via the Crypto API.
- the lz4 module provided by the kernel does not allow to specify a compression level.

--- Message (James Simmons, 2025-01-15 17:57:14.000000000) ---
Patch Set 3:

Again lets not have the ancient fscrypt used forever issue repeated.

1) Make it possible to build again the native kernel compression modules. This way with newer 
   kernels we can deal with kernel changes and keep in sync plus we can test against newer 
   kernels. That means making it treat all compression levels same for native kernel builds 
   since that  is a add on you have done.

2) Move your private driver into libcfs which is our version of OFED compact.ko. This way we
   know it exist and needs to be removed one day.

3) Please for the love of god tell me you are working with the upstream community on 
   implementing different compression levels. If you are post a link to the mailing list
   discussing the changes.

--- Message (Sebastien Buisson, 2025-01-16 09:28:51.000000000) ---
Patch Set 3:

> Patch Set 3:
> 
> Again lets not have the ancient fscrypt used forever issue repeated.
> 
> 1) Make it possible to build again the native kernel compression modules. This way with newer 
>    kernels we can deal with kernel changes and keep in sync plus we can test against newer 
>    kernels. That means making it treat all compression levels same for native kernel builds 
>    since that  is a add on you have done.

This is not like fscrypt, in the sense that we do not build Lustre _against_ any compression module. This Lustre copied lz4 module is 'just' built, and then Lustre uses the Crypto API to carry out compression with an algorithm named "lz4". If the kernel provides one, and if the Lustre provided one is not loaded, then the kernel's one will be used. We define the Lustre provided lz4 module with a higher priority so that it is preferred over the kernel's in case both are loaded, in order for the compression level to be taken into account.

So basically we are just adding a compression module, that happens to be lz4 plus some interface tweaks. But the Lustre code is not bound to it.

Please note that even on the latest CentOS 9 stream distro, the kernel config has:

   # CONFIG_CRYPTO_LZ4 is not set
   # CONFIG_CRYPTO_LZ4HC is not set

Which means lz4 is not available via the Crypto API interface. The Linux kernel certainly offers many capabilities, but if major distros disable them by default, we have to find another way around.

--- Message (Sebastien Buisson, 2025-01-17 15:59:05.000000000) ---
Patch Set 7:

> Patch Set 7:
> 
> The difficulty with testing the native client will be the extra work needed for the test framework to no load these modules since they will not be present. Its going to be a messy patch work. Oh wells.

James, I am not sure this is going to be that messy. Thanks to the Crypto API, compression modules are loaded when they are requested by their names. Lustre is going to use the common names ('lz4', 'zstd', ...) and not the driver names (e.g. 'lz4-lustre-generic'). So from Lustre kernel side, whatever module that provides the requested name will be used. And from the test framework perspective, if the "custom" compression modules are not available, it looks like the function 'load_compression_modules' will just fail but not forward the error, and setup will continue.

--- Message (Andreas Dilger, 2025-01-17 19:04:06.000000000) ---
Patch Set 8:

> I guess I will be ignored for my request for moving to libcfs. It
 > would be nice to just to have to load libcfs.

James, these are standalone CryptoAPI modules that are not linked directly into Lustre.  As such, I don't think it would be an improvement to merge them into libcfs. 

They are only loaded in demand if data compression is used, and are compatible with existing kernel CryptoAPI compression modules. We pass the compression level via a parameter that is unused for compression modules (it is used for salt or key or something for encryption modules, IIRC).

kunit/kcompr.c seems to be completely broken and only appears to work by pure chance. If you refresh it with e.g. vmalloc with no other changes I'll include it in the upcoming master-next landings still.

BTW I am a bit uneasy about just introducing a module that reads random files by userspace command from who knows what context. It does not look like these buffers ar written out, but we need to think how to clearly separate such test only kernel modules so they are not shipped with prod modules for anybody to trigger (and even better if they are not even built for prod builds so they don't get signed by kernel keys and the secure sites don't even have a chance of having working version of the even being accidentally installed)

--- Message (Timothy Day, 2025-06-17 21:03:38.000000000) ---
Patch Set 19:

I think loading a file via a kunit test is fine. The kunit module are already in their own test package (so separate from the actual filesystem modules). So users can opt to not install the test code if they have no use for it. And if a user has the permission to load a kernel module in the first place, it doesn't really matter if the kunit modules are there or not IMHO.

--- Message (Patrick Farrell, 2026-09-11 14:56:16.000000000) ---
Patch Set 32:

I built this on top of the current CSDC series and tested it on a single-node EL9 OSS.  The modules work -- llz4.ko and llz4hc.ko build, auto-load via the crypto-llz4 alias, register at priority 110, and make the compression level real, which the kernel's own algorithms do not.  Three things worth fixing before it lands.

1. The registered names do not match what Lustre asks for.

This patch registers cra_name "lz4" and "lz4hc".  crypto_name_from_type() in lustre/obdclass/lustre_compr.c asks for:

    case LL_COMPR_TYPE_LZ4FAST:
            return "lz4fast";
    case LL_COMPR_TYPE_LZ4:
            if (level < LZ4HC_MIN_CLEVEL - 1)
                    return "lz4fast";
            return "lz4";

Nothing registers "lz4fast" -- not this patch, not the kernel (no such name in crypto/ or lib/lz4 in either 5.14-rhel9 or 6.12-rhel10), and the only cra_names anywhere in the tree with this applied are lz4, lz4hc and adler32.  So -Z lz4fast returns -ENOENT and falls back to LZO even with both modules loaded:

    LustreError: __alloc_compr()) Cannot initialize compressor 4, error -2.
    Lustre: ...: type 4 crypto setup failed, try LZO(6): rc = -2

And LL_COMPR_TYPE_LZ4 asks for "lz4", the acceleration-factor algorithm, so lz4hc is never requested by any path.  I confirmed that by removing llz4.ko while leaving llz4hc.ko loaded: -Z lz4 then failed -ENOENT rather than using lz4hc.

This is wider than an explicit -Z lz4fast, because ldlm_lib.c sets cl_compr_type_fast = LL_COMPR_TYPE_LZ4FAST, so plain -Z fast hits the same dead name and silently becomes LZO.

The surrounding logic is already right: LZ4HC_MIN_CLEVEL is 3 and to_lz4_level() is level + 1, so "level < LZ4HC_MIN_CLEVEL - 1" correctly picks plain lz4 when the level is below what lz4hc accepts.  Only the two names are shifted by one notch:

    case LL_COMPR_TYPE_LZ4FAST:
            return "lz4";
    case LL_COMPR_TYPE_LZ4:
            if (level < LZ4HC_MIN_CLEVEL - 1)
                    return "lz4";
            return "lz4hc";

With that, no fallbacks are logged for any type, and lz4 becomes the best of the family instead of the worst (1192 KiB, against 1500-1976 KiB for lz4fast at various levels, on the same payload).

66001 takes the other approach for zstd and registers "zstdfast" to match the existing name.  Either is fine, but the lz4 pair currently matches neither.

2. It no longer applies to master.  It patches Makefile.in and config/Rules.in, both replaced by the top-level Kbuild; the hook is now "obj-m += lustre_compat/crypto/" in Kbuild.

3. to_lz4_level() in lustre_compat/crypto/lz4/lz4.h now collides with the to_lz4_level() the CSDC series added to lustre_user.h, so the build fails with "redefinition of 'to_lz4_level'".  They also mean different things -- the vendored one maps a level to an LZ4 acceleration factor, the uapi one to an lz4hc compression level -- so renaming one of them is needed regardless.

--- Message (Lustre RISC-V Builder, 2026-09-11 15:49:11.000000000) ---
Patch Set 32:

RISC-V Client Builder: build succesful

--- Message (Patrick Farrell, 2026-09-14 15:20:26.000000000) ---
Patch Set 32:

Follow-up to my earlier comment, with the concrete changes rather than just the problems.  I carry a rebased version of this patch locally that builds against current master and passes sanity-compr, and the delta is small enough to list.

1.  to_lz4_level() collision.

lustre_compat/crypto/lz4/lz4.h defines to_lz4_level(), and the CSDC series has since added a different to_lz4_level() to lustre_user.h, so the build fails with "redefinition of to_lz4_level".  They are not the same function: the vendored one maps a level to an LZ4 acceleration factor (1-9 as-is, then steps of 3), the uapi one maps a level to an lz4hc compression level (level + 1).

I renamed the vendored one, which keeps the uapi name for the thing user tools already use:

    -static inline u8 to_lz4_level(u8 level)
    +static inline u8 lz4_accel_from_level(u8 level)

and its single caller in lz4.c.

2.  The level macros are already in the tree.

lustre_crypto.h now carries COMPR_LEVEL_SHIFT / COMPR_LEVEL_MASK (and LGZIP_MOD_NAME / LZSTD_MOD_NAME alongside LLZ4FAST_MOD_NAME / LLZ4HC_MOD_NAME), so this patch's LZ4COMPR_LEVEL_SHIFT / LZ4COMPR_LEVEL_MASK are duplicates under a different name.  Dropping the patch's versions and using the existing ones in lz4.c and lz4hc.c is enough.

3.  Makefile.in no longer exists.

The build hook moved to the top-level Kbuild:

    obj-m += lustre_compat/crypto/

placed after "obj-m += lustre/".

4.  The config/Rules.in hunk no longer applies.

config/Rules.in is gone, and its "subdir-ccflags-y += $(EXTRA_CFLAGS)" is not needed: the top-level Kbuild already sets subdir-ccflags-y for the whole tree, and it reaches lustre_compat/ because that is under the same M= build.

The autoMakefile.am hunk (modulefs_DATA from find lustre_compat/ -name *.ko) still applies unchanged.

With those four, it builds on 5.14.0-611 EL9, llz4.ko and llz4hc.ko load and register lz4 and lz4hc at priority 110, and sanity-compr passes.  I am happy to push this as a new patchset if that is easier than folding it in -- say the word.
